Report to moderator Quick Reply Reply Quote Thank You Mastering price reading 15 Jul 2017 16:26 #13 Enlightenme --> Offline Trader Posts: 180 Thank you received: 137 Mukhcin wrote: a loss trade...i can't find a reason of FO. and is it and eng or FO...? It seems like you got the direction right that it is going up. FO areas are there to trick retailers. Just like what IF implied- when ITs want to sell, they need retailers to buy. Not all FLs work and FTB is only for those FLs that work. I used to think that breaking important areas create a strong bounce during FTB to the strong FL made. However, it doesn't seem to be the case. In my opinion, the FL reliability doesn't depend on the importance of the area it broke, but on the importance of the area it is in. Of course, watch for PA. Try watching the RTMA lite videos and the other lengthy videos. They helped me a lot. :) -EM Check this box to be notified of replies to this topic. Note: BBcode and smileys are still usable. Report to moderator Quick Reply Reply Quote Thank You Mastering price reading 13 Aug 2017 12:57 #97 staurum --> Offline Master Trader Posts: 940 Thank you received: 1954 Just one note: not saying that your drawing could be wrong, but be really careful on drawing the extent/width of the CPPAZ, because there is the start of what you're drawing right-afterwards. One fraction of less or more width of the zone will definitely affect the zones you draw on the rightside of it. Usually for me though, if not sure, i would suggest to have different scenarios of what-ifs and on HTF which one fits more logically to what ITs are doing (i.e. attracting retailers money to their accounts). Report to moderator Quick Reply Reply Quote Thank You Mastering price reading 19 Oct 2017 22:15 #184 Rinehart --> Away Associate Trader Posts: 450 Thank you received: 927 Mukhcin wrote: einrikr wrote: How annoying! And how come? It clearly got hit, was spread at that time higher then now? i don't know about the spread, but move was at least some 2.5RR before further move down... In Forex trading there is much less regulation than in equities for example. So with fewer rules, the banks will use every tool at their disposal to take our money or in this case not allow you into a trade that will cost them money. During every news release they have an excuse to widen the spread. The more important the news the better the excuse they have. If you are executing a long position, you need to be aware that these positions are even harder to catch because they normally widen the ask. They do this because our charts print based on the bid price and it is better to hide their manipulation. Here is a chart of today I picked. You can see the spread goes as high as 6 pips at times with this broker. Granted this is daily rollover when it gets this high. However the spread increases multiple times a day---news releases, equity market openings, market closings, slower times, faster times, etc, etc, etc. Now here is another chart for you. This is a screenshot I took when I got 360 pips of slippage on USDCAD. I, of course, contacted the broker and their answer as always is that it is not them but the liquidity providers. Don't be fooled. * Be aware when trading around things like Central Bank Rate announcements. * Beware trading around end of day rollover or even worse end of week. * Understand the more "exotic" a pair the wider the spread can go without warning. NZD normally has higher spread than EUR and in some cases, IT will go absolutely nuts on the spread around things like end of day rollover. * Recognize that long trades at fantastic areas might need an extra pip or two to get filled. Report to moderator Quick Reply Reply Quote Thank You Mastering price reading 17 Nov 2017 19:10 #192 dengaima --> Offline Trader Posts: 362 Thank you received: 305 PinkPanter wrote: i have been tricked a lot of times out of great position with this spread trick... Please help me understand this bid/ask better... I think in scenario 1 your trade is dead the moment ask price widens 30 pips Scenario 2 will not execute the trade Explanation: those price is not related to your condition but other party's condition. Bid is someone willing to pay you (when you sell) Ask is someone willing to offer to you (when you buy) Simplified: When you look for SL placement. Watch out for line closer to you. Buy execute - look out for ask line hitting your SL Sell execute - look out for bid line hitting your SL When setting limit orders - watch the line which is further from you For sell limits the bid line has to touch your limit line For buy limit your ask line has to touch your limit line In conclusion look for line which is disadvantageous to u. Report to moderator Quick Reply Reply Quote Thank You Mastering price reading 15 Jul 2017 16:26 #13 Enlightenme --> Offline Trader Posts: 180 Thank you received: 137 Mukhcin wrote: a loss trade...i can't find a reason of FO. and is it and eng or FO...? It seems like you got the direction right that it is going up. FO areas are there to trick retailers. Just like what IF implied- when ITs want to sell, they need retailers to buy. Not all FLs work and FTB is only for those FLs that work. I used to think that breaking important areas create a strong bounce during FTB to the strong FL made. However, it doesn't seem to be the case. In my opinion, the FL reliability doesn't depend on the importance of the area it broke, but on the importance of the area it is in. Of course, watch for PA. Try watching the RTMA lite videos and the other lengthy videos. They helped me a lot. :) -EM Check this box to be notified of replies to this topic. Note: BBcode and smileys are still usable. Report to moderator Quick Reply Reply Quote Thank You Mastering price reading 13 Aug 2017 12:57 #97 staurum --> Offline Master Trader Posts: 940 Thank you received: 1954 Just one note: not saying that your drawing could be wrong, but be really careful on drawing the extent/width of the CPPAZ, because there is the start of what you're drawing right-afterwards. One fraction of less or more width of the zone will definitely affect the zones you draw on the rightside of it. Usually for me though, if not sure, i would suggest to have different scenarios of what-ifs and on HTF which one fits more logically to what ITs are doing (i.e. attracting retailers money to their accounts). Report to moderator Quick Reply Reply Quote Thank You Mastering price reading 19 Oct 2017 22:15 #184 Rinehart --> Away Associate Trader Posts: 450 Thank you received: 927 Mukhcin wrote: einrikr wrote: How annoying! And how come? It clearly got hit, was spread at that time higher then now? i don't know about the spread, but move was at least some 2.5RR before further move down... In Forex trading there is much less regulation than in equities for example. So with fewer rules, the banks will use every tool at their disposal to take our money or in this case not allow you into a trade that will cost them money. During every news release they have an excuse to widen the spread. The more important the news the better the excuse they have. If you are executing a long position, you need to be aware that these positions are even harder to catch because they normally widen the ask. They do this because our charts print based on the bid price and it is better to hide their manipulation. Here is a chart of today I picked. You can see the spread goes as high as 6 pips at times with this broker. Granted this is daily rollover when it gets this high. However the spread increases multiple times a day---news releases, equity market openings, market closings, slower times, faster times, etc, etc, etc. Now here is another chart for you. This is a screenshot I took when I got 360 pips of slippage on USDCAD. I, of course, contacted the broker and their answer as always is that it is not them but the liquidity providers. Don't be fooled. * Be aware when trading around things like Central Bank Rate announcements. * Beware trading around end of day rollover or even worse end of week. * Understand the more "exotic" a pair the wider the spread can go without warning. NZD normally has higher spread than EUR and in some cases, IT will go absolutely nuts on the spread around things like end of day rollover. * Recognize that long trades at fantastic areas might need an extra pip or two to get filled. Report to moderator Quick Reply Reply Quote Thank You Mastering price reading 17 Nov 2017 19:10 #192 dengaima --> Offline Trader Posts: 362 Thank you received: 305 PinkPanter wrote: i have been tricked a lot of times out of great position with this spread trick... Please help me understand this bid/ask better... I think in scenario 1 your trade is dead the moment ask price widens 30 pips Scenario 2 will not execute the trade Explanation: those price is not related to your condition but other party's condition. Bid is someone willing to pay you (when you sell) Ask is someone willing to offer to you (when you buy) Simplified: When you look for SL placement. Watch out for line closer to you. Buy execute - look out for ask line hitting your SL Sell execute - look out for bid line hitting your SL When setting limit orders - watch the line which is further from you For sell limits the bid line has to touch your limit line For buy limit your ask line has to touch your limit line In conclusion look for line which is disadvantageous to u.
